## cam/cam-run-workspace — cam run persistent workspace

- **Started**: 2026-05-30
- **Closed**: 2026-06-06
- **Branch**: cam/cam-run-workspace
- **Issue**: none
- **Outcome**: shipped
- **Summary**: Introduced `cam run` as the canonical operator entry point. Creates a persistent tmux session with three panes: orchestrator, dashboard, and interactive menu. All subcommands (next, plan, issue) become pane launchers inside the shared session. Four hardening stories (R1-001 to R1-004) fixed stable pane IDs, shell injection, stale help text, and a misleading fallback message.
- **Decisions**: Design tokens for run menu colors (keeps TUI consistent with Ink dashboard); argv-based pane launch to fix shell injection.
- **Blockers encountered**: Bun arm64 binaries need ad-hoc codesign re-signing when installed to /usr/local/bin (amfid kills the process). Documented in lessons.md 2026-06-06.
- **Follow-ups**: Add .claude/.cam-run-menu.sh to .gitignore (generated runtime file). US-010 operator smoke left as manual ceremony.

## cam/CAM-48-responsive-dashboard-pane: responsive cam-run dashboard/menu column

- **Started**: 2026-06-14
- **Closed**: 2026-06-14
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-48-responsive-dashboard-pane
- **Issue**: CAM-48
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #45)
- **Summary**: The cam-run tmux right column (dashboard + menu) split at a fixed -l 36 against the -x 220 virtual session, collapsing below readable width on narrow clients (observed ~20 cols on a 188-col laptop, reproduced live in the orchestrator's own session). Fix: clampDashboardWidth(w) = clamp(round(w*0.20), 34, 52); born-clamped split (44 at 220) plus a per-session window-resized hook that re-clamps the dashboard pane (the menu pane shares the column) via shell $(()) on every resize.
- **Decisions**: The clamp must use shell arithmetic, not pure tmux format. Verified empirically on tmux 3.6a: resize-pane -x rejects #{...} format expressions, and tmux comparison modifiers (#{>:a,b}) are lexical, not numeric. The hook reads #{window_width} via run-shell (tmux expands it before sh -c) and computes the clamped literal with $(()). Hook is best-effort (|| true) so a failed resize never crashes the session.
- **Blockers encountered**: Review round 1 caught a round-vs-truncate drift: the hook used w*20/100 (floor) while clampDashboardWidth uses Math.round, so window_width 188 gave 37 not 38, violating the code's own single-source-of-truth comment. Fixed in US-R1-001 with round-half-up (w*20+50)/100. Round 2 verdict CLEAN.
- **Follow-ups**: US-004 (operator) verified by objective pane-width measurement (operator-authorized flip), final eyeball deferred to the next post-merge cam run. Cosmetic doc-comment still says "-L cam socket" (skipped, not worth a re-review round). Process note: `git rm a b missing.txt` is atomic, one missing path aborts the whole removal; the ship hygiene step's `git rm prd.json handoff.json progress.txt 2>/dev/null || true` silently dropped nothing because progress.txt was already retired.

## cam/CAM-49-cam-ship-hygiene-ignore-unmatch: cam-ship Step 4b git rm robustness

- **Started**: 2026-06-14
- **Closed**: 2026-06-14
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-49-cam-ship-hygiene-ignore-unmatch
- **Issue**: CAM-49
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #46)
- **Summary**: Fixes the exact process-note bug logged at the end of the CAM-48 entry. /cam-ship Step 4b dropped per-branch state with `git rm -q prd.json handoff.json progress.txt 2>/dev/null || true`. git rm is atomic over its pathspec list: progress.txt was retired in CAM-31, so every ship since aborted the whole removal touching nothing, and the mask hid it, leaking prd.json/handoff.json to main (defeating CAM-27) and making /cam-plan Step 1 read a stale prd.json on main. Fix: `git rm -q --ignore-unmatch ...` (a missing path is benign, exit 0) with the `2>/dev/null || true` mask dropped so genuine git errors fail loud. Applied to both the templates/ copy (the cam init target) and this repo's .claude/ dogfood copy, plus the regenerated src/vendor/_generated.ts.
- **Decisions**: Keep progress.txt in the pathspec (harmless under --ignore-unmatch, defensive for ancient branches). The ship command is markdown executed by an LLM with no TS code path, so the only automatable regression guard is a test on the embedded templatesContents map (what cam init writes into user projects): that is US-002, which pins the robust form and forbids the masked-atomic pattern.
- **Blockers encountered**: PRD audit BLOCKed once: issueNumber was the string "CAM-49" instead of the bare integer 49, which would make Step 4a construct id "CAM-CAM-49" and silently fail to close the issue. Fixed to 49 (matches every prior shipped PRD and the planner schema); auditor then APPROVEd. Review round 1 verdict CLEAN.
- **Follow-ups**: none. The fix dogfooded itself on its own ship: with progress.txt absent, the fixed Step 4b exited 0 and staged prd.json + handoff.json for deletion (verified via git show --stat), so this cycle's harness state did not leak to main. Closes the CAM-48 process-note loop.

## cam/CAM-56-two-layer-verification: two-layer verification (gates + reviewer binary rubric)

- **Started**: 2026-06-16
- **Closed**: 2026-06-16
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-56-two-layer-verification
- **Issue**: CAM-56
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #57)
- **Summary**: Two-layer verification modeled on Jaymin West. Layer A (implementer): a gate is a named command + exitCode 0 with a success/partial/failure roll-up, and the implementer is PROHIBITED from declaring a story done before gates pass. Layer B (reviewer): binary PASS/FAIL on a FIXED 8-criterion rubric with per-criterion cited evidence plus a does-not-trust-green-tests clause. Planner pairs each acceptanceCriterion with an oracle (named-command, file-assert, or reviewer-judgment); auditor BLOCKs on any criterion lacking one. Prompt/doc/template/embed/test only, no runtime code. 947 tests.
- **Decisions**: kept the existing gates {typecheck,tests} shape (US-003) instead of extending it; preserved the <review> sentinel + parseReviewVerdict (review path untouched).
- **Blockers encountered**: dogfood blocked on CAM-57 (worker-pane never created post-CAM-55 2-pane mutex). Fixed via PR #55 (sidecar self-heal) + rebuild/reinstall + in-place sidecar respawn, no session restart. CAM-58 (supervisor false-terminals after each correct story: readWorkerOutcome reads handoff.lastCompletedStory.id but the push-model worker writes a string, no worker-report.json fallback) forced manual per-story re-arms, and once all stories passed the review could not be sidecar-dispatched (hasPendingStories=false), so review ran via /cam-review directly. Round 1 FIXES_PENDING:1 (the new rubric caught a real cite error: parseReviewVerdict cited at result.ts vs the real review.ts:151), round 2 CLEAN.
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-58 (filed). Reviewer round-1 backlog: tighten the trivially-satisfied US-002 BLOCK test assertion; em-dash drift in the auditor prompt; the implementer template leaks the cam-cli-specific worker-report.ts path.

## cam/CAM-58-supervisor-outcome-worker-report: supervisor outcome worker-report fallback + review dispatch

- **Started**: 2026-06-17
- **Closed**: 2026-06-17
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-58-supervisor-outcome-worker-report
- **Issue**: CAM-58
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #59)
- **Summary**: Fixes the CAM-56-dogfood false-terminal. readWorkerOutcome now falls back to the authoritative worker-report.json and tolerates a string lastCompletedStory (US-001); implementer writes lastCompletedStory as the {id,title} object the schema requires (US-002); sidecar makeHasPendingStories counts a non-terminal review as pending so review auto-dispatches when all stories pass (US-003, also exported for tests); staleness guard so the fallback only trusts a report matching the dispatched story, avoiding a false-pass (US-004); structured outcome-fallback event (US-005); real-fs integration regression reproducing the exact failure shape (US-006). The branch was built end-to-end by the cam loop itself: US-001..US-006 ran back-to-back with ZERO manual re-arm, the supervisor auto-dispatched the reviewer when the last non-operator story passed, review returned CLEAN round 1, 976 tests / 0 fail.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7 stories) chosen by the operator over MVP (3). US-007 (operator dogfood) accepted as satisfied by this very run, since the loop demonstrably self-drove story to review with no re-arm.
- **Blockers encountered**: The planner output passed audit (APPROVE) but the auditor flagged 3 important trivially-satisfiable oracles, the exact class CAM-56 two-layer verification exists to catch: US-003 referenced test/commands/sidecar.test.ts (does not exist; canonical is test/supervisor/sidecar-loop.test.ts), makeHasPendingStories was module-private so a unit test could not import it, and US-006's grep did not forbid a shadow re-mock of the functions under test. All six findings (F-01..F-06) were applied before branching; the implemented US-003 landed in the correct canonical test file, confirming the fix took. Honest caveat: the live loop ran on the PRE-FIX binary (cam 0.1.2). The false-terminal is intermittent (fires only when the DONE sentinel escapes capture-pane) and did not trigger this run, so the live run validated the loop UX, not the fix; fix-correctness is carried by US-006's regression test against the real readWorkerOutcome.
- **Follow-ups**: Post-merge rebuild + reinstall so the operator binary carries the CAM-58 fix (US-007 AC #1, deferred). The dogfood ran the whole cycle clean, so the CAM-56 entry's re-arm friction is resolved in practice. The CAM-56 reviewer round-1 backlog (US-002 BLOCK test assertion, auditor em-dash, implementer template worker-report.ts path leak) is still open.

## cam/CAM-67-stop-kills-sidecar: cam stop kills the sidecar + cleans all markers; sidecar self-exit

- **Started**: 2026-06-17
- **Closed**: 2026-06-17
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-67-stop-kills-sidecar
- **Issue**: CAM-67
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #60)
- **Summary**: Fixes the dogfood sidecar leak (orphan cam sidecar processes survived cam stop and abnormal session exits, polling forever and piling up; one observed at ~11h uptime across sessions). Three mechanisms: cam run persists the sidecar pid at spawn to .claude/.cam-sidecar.pid so cam stop can SIGTERM it under a signal-0 liveness guard (US-001); cam stop now removes the full per-session marker set via the canonical constants (promoting .cam-orch-session to a new ORCH_SESSION_MARKER), idempotently (US-002); the sidecar self-terminates within one idle interval when tmux has-session misses its own session, with a startup grace against the session-creation race (US-003). Plus a structured sidecar-exit event and StopReport teardown fields (US-005), a cwd-scoped fallback kill that reaps sidecars orphaned before this fix (US-006), a recovery-runbook update (US-004), and a real-Bun.spawn + real-tmux integration regression (US-007). 995 tests / 0 fail, review CLEAN round 1.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7 stories) over MVP (4), chosen by the operator. The durable spawn-time pid file is required because .cam-supervisor.lock is held only while the sidecar is mid-supervision (acquired in runSidecarLoop's active branch, released in finally), so an idle orphan holds no lock and the lock pid alone cannot find it. US-006's scoped reaper is what kills the already-loose sidecars; US-001..005 only prevent new ones.
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the whole 7-story PRD self-drove story to story to review with ZERO manual re-arm, the CAM-58 false-terminal fix holding live end to end (US-007 ran as a normal automated integration test, not an operator ceremony). Plan-time: the auditor returned APPROVE with 1 important + 3 suggestion findings, all applied before branching. The important one (F-01) was an inverted-grep oracle in US-002: a positive grep asserting marker literals are PRESENT where a negative grep forbidding raw literals was meant, which would have rubber-stamped a non-constant implementation. Fixed to a negative grep plus a separate positive constants-imported assertion, and .cam-orch-session was promoted to ORCH_SESSION_MARKER so the negative grep is not self-contradictory.
- **Follow-ups**: Post-merge rebuild + reinstall so the operator binary carries the CAM-67 fix (the live run still drove on cam 0.1.2). CAM-65 (worker-pane closes on completion) still open: the reviewer pane lingers after a CLEAN verdict, harmless but not auto-recycled. The tracked .claude/.cam-orchestrator-prompt.txt churns once per session (cam run rewrites it); candidate to gitignore so it stops showing as a dirty working-tree file every cycle.

## cam/CAM-69-smoke-zero-dep: zero-dep check-agent-frontmatter smoke

- **Started**: 2026-06-23
- **Closed**: 2026-06-23
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-69-smoke-zero-dep
- **Issue**: CAM-69
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #62)
- **Summary**: The vendored check-agent-frontmatter smoke imported js-yaml as a bare specifier. In a node_modules-less tmpdir Bun auto-installed js-yaml@5 (ESM-only, default export removed), so the smoke died with "Missing 'default' export" and two init.test.ts cases went red (cam init runs the smoke in a tmpdir). Fix: hand-rolled zero-dep frontmatter parser in vendor/check-agent-frontmatter.ts (US-001), a deterministic empty-cache tmpdir regression test that forces any accidental auto-install to fail offline-equivalent (US-002), and the v4/v5-safe named import in status.ts plus a CAM-69 recovery-runbook note (US-003). 996 pass / 0 fail, embed-vendor in parity, review CLEAN round 1.
- **Decisions**: Chose option (c) zero-dep hand-rolled parser over pinning or embedding js-yaml, since the agent frontmatter is intentionally simple (top-level key:scalar plus simple tools/disallowedTools lists). Real correctness oracle is bun test, not typecheck (vendor/ is excluded from tsconfig by design).
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the loop self-drove US-001 to US-002 to US-003 to reviewer with zero manual re-arm (CAM-58 false-terminal fix holding live). Plan-time: the auditor flagged trivially-satisfiable oracles (F-01..F-06), all applied before branching.
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-68 (gitignore the escaped harness runtime markers) still open: this ship staged selectively to keep .cam-orchestrator-prompt.txt churn and the untracked .cam-orch-*/.cam-sidecar.pid out of the PR. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers after CLEAN) still open.

## cam/CAM-68-gitignore-runtime-markers: gitignore the escaped harness runtime markers (repo + template + test)

- **Started**: 2026-06-23
- **Closed**: 2026-06-23
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-68-gitignore-runtime-markers
- **Issue**: CAM-68
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #63)
- **Summary**: Seven cam runtime markers under .claude/ had escaped the .gitignore covering their siblings, so every cam run dirtied the working tree (and /cam-prune aborts on a dirty tree). Operator approved the full sweep over the 3-marker MVP: the issue named .cam-orchestrator-prompt.txt / .cam-orch-ready / .cam-sidecar.pid and flagged the setup pair as latent; the two handoff markers (.cam-orch-handoff.json, .cam-orch-handoff.consumed.json) were missed by the investigation but are the same class. US-001 added 7 rules to the repo .gitignore + git rm --cached the 3 tracked (.cam-orchestrator-prompt.txt, .cam-setup-menu.sh, .cam-setup-prompt.txt); US-002 mirrored the 7 rules into templates/.gitignore + regenerated src/vendor/_generated.ts (embed-vendor:check parity); US-003 pinned all 7 in the embedded templatesContents map + a git-state guard against re-tracking. 1004 pass / 0 fail, review CLEAN round 1. The fix dogfooded itself: the markers left this session's working tree the moment US-001 landed.
- **Decisions**: Full sweep (3 stories, 7 markers) over MVP (3 markers), chosen by the operator. Cover the class, not the instance (CAM-49 precedent).
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the cycle self-drove plan-approval to review with ZERO manual re-arm (CAM-58 false-terminal fix holding live again). Ship hygiene hit a NEW variant of the CAM-48/CAM-49 git rm class: prd.json carried the supervisor-written review block as an uncommitted modification at ship time, so Step 4b's `git rm -q --ignore-unmatch` (no -f) refused it on the local-modifications guard and the atomic rm aborted, leaving handoff.json tracked too; the subsequent `git add -A` then re-committed prd.json modified instead of removing it (would have leaked per-branch state to main, defeating CAM-27). Fixed in-cycle by `git rm -f --ignore-unmatch` + commit --amend before push.
- **Follow-ups**: File a CAM issue to harden /cam-ship Step 4b (`git rm -f --ignore-unmatch`, or commit/stash the review block first, so a dirty prd.json at ship time cannot re-leak). CAM-70 (reviewer CLEAN verdict did not reach the orchestrator via send-keys; read from prd.review directly) confirmed live again. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) still open.

## cam/CAM-72-deterministic-cycle-close: deterministic /cam-ship cycle-close (cam ship --finalize)

- **Started**: 2026-06-23
- **Closed**: 2026-06-23
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-72-deterministic-cycle-close
- **Issue**: CAM-72
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #64)
- **Summary**: Closes the exact follow-up the CAM-68 entry filed. Moves the /cam-ship cycle-close hygiene out of LLM-executed markdown into deterministic TypeScript: new finalizeCycleClose() (src/commands/ship-finalize.ts), invoked via `cam ship --finalize` which branches before the thin-proxy (US-002). It reads the issue backend, closes the local issue in issues.local.json, removes per-branch state (prd.json, handoff.json, progress.txt) with `git rm -f --ignore-unmatch`, and commits in one cycle-close commit; markdown Step 4b is now a single invocation (US-003, both copies + embed-vendor). US-004 is the real-git regression the issue demanded (dirty prd.json ends DELETED, not modified), US-005 hardening/idempotency, US-006 a structured result line (for CAM-71 auto-ship narration), US-007 the recovery-runbook. 1032 pass / 0 fail.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7 stories) over MVP (4), operator choice. Core fix: `git rm -f --ignore-unmatch`, the -f overrides git's local-modifications guard that plain --ignore-unmatch does not. Git surface uses DI'd synchronous spawn (mirrors status.ts / resume.ts) over Bun.spawn for hermetic tests, a documented deviation flagged by the auditor (F-04) and accepted.
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the loop self-drove plan-approval through 7 stories to review with ZERO manual re-arm (CAM-58 holding live). Review round 1 returned FIXES_PENDING on a real find (US-003 left a stale "Step 4a" cross-reference in cam-ship.md Step 7), fixed in US-R1-001, round 2 CLEAN. The CLEAN verdict did not reach the orchestrator via send-keys (CAM-70 still open), read from prd.review directly. Plan-time the auditor APPROVEd with 5 suggestions, 3 applied before branching (F-01 em-dash oracle on both copies, F-02 behavioral thin-proxy oracle, F-04 spawn-deviation note).
- **Follow-ups**: The fix dogfooded itself on its own ship: prd.json was dirty with the review block, and `cam ship --finalize` (run via `bun index.ts`, commit 1f5abc2) deleted prd.json + handoff.json rather than re-committing the modified prd.json, verified by git ls-files empty + clean tree. Post-merge rebuild + reinstall so the operator binary carries the --finalize flag (installed cam 0.1.x lacks it). CAM-71 (auto-ship) now unblocked on its deterministic-close dependency. CAM-70 and CAM-65 still open.

## cam/CAM-59-ci-macos-gate-spine: CI on macOS + deterministic gate spine (check:all/verify + ci-parity)

- **Started**: 2026-06-23
- **Closed**: 2026-06-23
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-59-ci-macos-gate-spine
- **Issue**: CAM-59
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #65)
- **Summary**: cam's first CI (the agent-readiness keystone). macos-latest GitHub Actions (setup-bun@v2, brew install tmux, bun install, bun run check:all) so the OS-gated real-tmux integration tests actually run in CI, not just on the laptop (Burrow's lesson). Plus the os-eco gate spine: a GATES-as-data check:all/verify runner (typecheck, test, embed-vendor, ci-parity; quiet ok/fail+timing, --bail, --json to GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY) and a check:ci-parity gate that fails if ci.yml drifts from the local manifest (green-local==green-CI). Launch-ready, 7 stories (US-006 operator). 1079 pass / 0 fail, review CLEAN.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (6) over MVP (3), operator choice. ci-parity manifest entry deferred from US-002 to US-003 (after ci.yml exists) so check:all never breaks between stories (auditor F-02). US-007 added mid-cycle, operator-approved, to keep "CI green" honest in one PR.
- **Blockers encountered**: The CI proved itself on day 1: the first run (red, 36s) caught a pre-existing green-local != green-CI bug. test/init.test.ts asserted runInit() exitCode 0, which only holds with claude on PATH; on the runner (no claude) runInit returns 1 though config.toml is written. US-007 made the 2 cases hermetic; second run (28057929789) green and the tmux-introspect suite ran with real 0.5 to 1.4s durations (not skipped), validating the Burrow's-lesson fix. Repo is PUBLIC, not private, so branch protection IS available (the issue's "free private plan" premise was wrong); corrected the CAM-59 note and filed CAM-73. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) hit the 2-pane mutex on the US-007 re-arm; killed the pane by hand. Binary 0.1.2 lacks the CAM-72 --finalize flag, so cycle-close ran via `bun index.ts ship --finalize` (sha 509e3ac).
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-73 (enable real branch protection now the repo is public + fix the agent-readiness.md branch_protection premise). CAM-65 (reviewer pane recycle) still open, hit again this cycle. CAM-70 (verdict via send-keys) read from prd.review directly again. Post-merge rebuild + reinstall so the binary carries --finalize (and CAM-58/67/68). Operator to merge PR #65.

## cam/CAM-60-static-layer-ratchets: linter (biome) + ratchets (file-size, coverage, debt-marker, dead-code, dup) on the check:all spine

- **Started**: 2026-06-23
- **Closed**: 2026-06-23
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-60-static-layer-ratchets
- **Issue**: CAM-60
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #66, CI green)
- **Summary**: The fleet-canonical static layer on top of CAM-59's check:all gate spine. Six new gates, each self-wired into the GATES manifest so they auto-propagate to CI through the single `bun run check:all` step (no per-gate ci.yml edits, ci-parity enforced): biome lint-only (US-001, recommended:false, only noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity + noExcessiveLinesPerFunction as errors, oversized files grandfathered via per-file overrides.includes, no catch-all), file-size ratchet down-only (US-002, raising a ceiling requires a tracker ref in the staged diff), debt-marker gate (US-003, every TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX must cite a tracker id; vendor/dist/harness excluded), coverage ratchet up-only (US-004, lowering a floor requires a tracker ref), knip dead-code (US-005), jscpd dup <= 4% (US-006), wiring + observability + recovery docs (US-007). Ratchet scripts mirror check-all.ts's injectable SpawnFn so unit tests never shell out. 1158 pass / 0 fail at ship, review CLEAN round 1.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7 stories) over MVP, operator choice; biome lint-only (no formatter) + lean ruleset (the two named complexity rules only) confirmed at plan time. Coverage ratchet floor-comparison tolerance set to 0.5pp (epsilon) over round-down-to-integer or just-lower-the-floors, operator choice, after CI red (below).
- **Blockers encountered**: The CI proved itself a second time (after CAM-59): the first run (red, 37s, run 28067359061) caught a real green-local != green-CI bug in the coverage ratchet (US-004). The floors were snapshotted at the exact local measurement (functions 75.65% / lines 78.63%) with zero margin; the macos runner measures ~0.2-0.3pp lower (75.37 / 78.43) because env-sensitive tests cover different lines (the literal CAM-59 lesson: init.test.ts without claude on PATH, OS-gated tmux tests), so strict `measured < floor` failed. Fixed by adding a 0.5pp FLOOR_TOLERANCE_PP to compareCoverageToFloors (floors unchanged at 75.65/78.63, so the lowering-requires-ref rule stays untriggered; error messages still report the raw floor) plus 2 new band tests; second run (green, 39s, run 28067914722) on the exact HEAD sha, PR MERGEABLE/CLEAN. The fix dogfooded the file-size ratchet: the epsilon comment + 2 tests pushed check-coverage.ts and its test over their ceilings, so the worker raised both per the runbook (j.1) with the tracker ref in file-size-budget.json `_ref`. At CI time the staged diff is empty (all committed) so the raised-ceiling rule is trivially satisfied. The CLEAN verdict did not reach the orchestrator via send-keys (CAM-70 still open), read from prd.review directly; reviewer pane lingered post-CLEAN (CAM-65).
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-70 (verdict via send-keys) and CAM-65 (reviewer pane recycle) both still open, hit again. The coverage-ratchet brittleness lesson (snapshot-on-adopt ratchets need a cross-environment tolerance, the CI measures coverage differently than local) is captured in scripts/cam/patterns.md. Operator to merge PR #66.

## cam/CAM-70-reviewer-verdict-handback: reviewer verdict handback to the orchestrator (sidecar push on review-done)

- **Started**: 2026-06-24
- **Closed**: 2026-06-24
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-70-reviewer-verdict-handback
- **Issue**: CAM-70
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #67, CI green 45s)
- **Summary**: Fixes the dogfood hang where the orchestrator sat at "wait for review" forever. Asymmetry from an incomplete CAM-55 conversion: the implementer pushes an exit-report to the orchestrator pane, the reviewer pushed nothing (the sidecar detected the verdict by polling capture-pane, updated prd.review, reached terminal, handed back nothing). Fix uses option (b): the SIDECAR pushes the deterministic terminal verdict via a new injectable notifyOrchestrator, called in the loop.ts review branch (and the blocked path, US-005), wired in host.ts through getOrchPaneId + the reused buildWorkerReportSendKeysArgv. Plus a structured handback event (US-004), a real-tmux integration test that the verdict line lands in the orch pane (US-003), and a recovery-runbook note (US-006). 1176 pass / 0 fail, review CLEAN.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7) over MVP (3), operator choice. Option (b) sidecar-push over (a) reviewer-agent-push: the sidecar computes the terminal verdict (CLEAN vs MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT) the reviewer never emits, parses deterministically without trusting the pane, and avoids reviewer-prompt drift. No new send-keys builder (reused buildWorkerReportSendKeysArgv, already correct re: the -l+Enter gotcha).
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the loop self-drove US-001..006 + review round 1 FIXES_PENDING:1 (US-R1-001 raised file-size ratchet ceilings for the CAM-70 additions, same class as CAM-60) + round 2 CLEAN, ZERO manual re-arm. Plan-time the auditor BLOCKed once on 1 critical + 2 important oracle defects, the exact two-layer-verification class: F-01 a structurally-broken em-dash pipeline (`! grep | grep` negates only the first stage), F-02 a trivially-satisfiable OS-gate grep, F-03 a tautological `review.*blocked` matching a pre-existing comment. All five findings applied, re-audit APPROVE. The bug reproduced live one last time: running on the unfixed 0.1.2 sidecar, the round-2 CLEAN verdict did NOT auto-reach the orchestrator, read from prd.review by hand (operator said "feito"). That live reproduction is the strongest evidence the fix was needed.
- **Follow-ups**: US-007 (operator dogfood) accepted via automated proof (US-003 real-tmux integration test + US-001/002 unit tests); the live end-to-end confirmation is deferred to a post-merge rebuild+reinstall, since the fixed binary was never the one driving this run. Post-merge rebuild+reinstall carries CAM-70 plus the still-missing CAM-58/67/68/72. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) still open, hit again (pane %2). CAM-71 (auto-ship) now unblocked on its handback prerequisite. Operator to merge PR #67.

## cam/CAM-53-cam-config-models-backend: cam config (model per phase + backend selector)

- **Closed**: 2026-06-24

- **Issue**: CAM-53

- **Summary**: The prerequisite for CAM-54 (codex backend). New `cam config` Ink menu picks model per phase + backend (claude default, codex selectable but guarded as not-yet-wired), persisted to project.toml [models]/[backend]. The 3 cam-spawned phases (orchestrator/implementer/reviewer) read --model from config; the 3 in-session phases (planner/auditor/ship) are frontmatter-governed, which cam config rewrites on save (US-006). Plus the opus-4-7 to opus-4-8 frontmatter quick-win (US-004), init defaults (US-005), spawn-resolution observability + codex guard (US-007), and cam config --show (US-008). 1265 pass / 0 fail, full check:all spine green, review CLEAN.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (9 stories) over MVP (5), operator choice. CAM-53 sequenced BEFORE CAM-54 (both specs name it as the prerequisite; operator confirmed). Key split surfaced at plan time: only orchestrator/implementer/reviewer take a runtime --model (cam controls their argv); planner/auditor/ship run in-session (Task/slash) so their model is frontmatter-governed (US-006). Config is project-scoped (scripts/cam/project.toml), NOT the per-user config; CAM_CONFIG_PATH not overloaded.
- **Blockers encountered**: Plan-time the auditor BLOCKed once with 10 findings (3 critical tautological/trivially-satisfiable oracles + a config-file precedence gap); all applied, re-audit APPROVE. Runtime: the loop self-drove US-001..008 clean, but the review did NOT converge in 3 rounds (3 to 1 to 1 findings). Root cause: the auto-generated fix stories (US-R*-001) are content-less placeholders, so the fix-worker gets a generic "implement next story" prompt with NO finding text and must re-derive it, producing a no-op US-R2-001; round 3 reincided and the loop dispatched a 4th fix (exceeding maxRounds:3). Soft-paused (active:false), discarded the timed-out US-R3-001 WIP, and spawned a clean reviewer to get the true finding: US-007's spawn-resolution event never persisted in production (emitSpawnResolution called without a writeEvent sink at all 3 call-sites; no spawn-resolution event kind in WorkerEventKind), breaking the US-009 evidence channel. Fixed manually (ba5f153): wired writeEvent via makeFileEventLogger at the 3 call-sites + a production-wiring test that fails if unwired; re-review CLEAN (8/8). Ship hygiene: the knip dead-code gate flaked on a corrupt bunx cache (CAM-69 redux, fixed by clearing it); the file-size ratchet caught 7 files grown past ceiling (the per-story worker runs only typecheck+tests, not check:all), raised to current counts with the CAM-53 _ref.
- **Follow-ups**: File 2 architectural issues surfaced by the review loop: (a) reviewer findings are NOT propagated into the auto-generated fix stories (fix-worker flies blind, no-ops), same class as CAM-58/70; (b) maxRounds is not a hard terminal (the loop dispatched a 4th fix round instead of declaring MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT). US-009 (operator dogfood) accepted via automated proof (US-002 argv tests + US-007 production-wiring test); live confirm deferred to post-merge rebuild+reinstall (installed cam predates CAM-53). CAM-54 (codex backend) now unblocked on its CAM-53 prerequisite. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) still open. Operator to merge PR #68, then prune + rebuild/reinstall.

## cam/CAM-75-reviewer-report-channel: reviewer findings via structured-report handback (review-report.json)

- **Started**: 2026-06-24
- **Closed**: 2026-06-25
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-75-reviewer-report-channel
- **Issue**: CAM-75
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #69, CI green 56s)
- **Summary**: Closes the CAM-53 follow-up (a): reviewer findings now reach the auto-generated fix stories. The original approach (parseReviewFindings over capturePane) was proven broken by a LIVE dogfood: tmux capture-pane returns the claude TUI's RENDERED markdown, so the `## CRITICAL` headers and `- [file:line]` bullets are consumed by the renderer and 0 findings extract (the `<review>` tag survives because it is not markdown syntax, which is why the verdict still parsed). Reframed to a structured-file handback: the reviewer writes scripts/cam/review-report.json {verdict, findings:[{severity,file,line,text}]}, the sidecar reads it for verdict+findings+completion, the `<review>` tag stays human/fallback only. 7 stories + 3 fix stories (R1x2, R2x1), 1286 pass / 0 fail, review CLEAN round 3.
- **Decisions**: Operator decided ALL inter-pane comms move to structured-file handback, single source per signal, sidecar sole pusher (5-concern model: Event=worker-report/review-report, State=prd.json, Context=handoff.json, Observability=event log, Wisdom=patterns.md). CAM-75 is slice 1; CAM-77 (implementer channel) + CAM-78 (push hygiene) filed. Rejected the transcript-source alternative (it couples to claude's internal jsonl layout + CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR resolution, the exact thing that bit the findings hunt) in favor of a reviewer-written file with an owned schema. handoff.json vs worker-report.json kept SEPARATE not merged: opposite lifecycles (durable/committed vs ephemeral/wiped-per-dispatch), different consumers (next agent vs sidecar).
- **Blockers encountered**: The first dogfood ran on the pre-fix binary and reproduced the bug live (findingsCount:0, placeholder fix stories, findings lost from the respawned pane); the blind fix-loop committed 2 real fixes before being reset. The fix dogfooded ITSELF: rebuilt the sidecar from the new code and re-ran review, which propagated findings correctly across 3 rounds (FIXES_PENDING:2 to :1 to CLEAN). The two-layer review independently caught 3 real gaps in the new code, including a clearReviewReport stale-read (review-report.json was not cleared before a review dispatch the way worker-report.json is at loop.ts:658) that I had anticipated; required a mid-loop rebuild before round 2 so the running sidecar carried the R1 fix and round 2 read a fresh report. The soft-pause via the state file did NOT hold (the sidecar owns and rewrites it); hard-kill of the sidecar process is the only reliable pause. Auditor BLOCKed once on 3 mechanically-broken oracles (grep -Lq exit-inverted x2, an em-dash literal-escape that never matches U+2014), fixed by the planner + re-audit APPROVE. Ship hygiene (CAM-60 class, recurring): the first CI run was RED on the file-size ratchet (review.ts 579, review.test.ts 1220, decide.ts 129, host.ts 645 over their ceilings) even though tests were 0 fail, because the per-story worker runs only typecheck+tests, not check:all; raised the 4 ceilings with the CAM-75 _ref + cleared a corrupt bunx knip cache (CAM-69 redux), ran check:all green locally, second CI run green. I declared shipped before CI confirmed, which was premature.
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-77 (implementer channel: worker-report.json as sole outcome source, collapse the readWorkerOutcome 3-source cross-check) and CAM-78 (sidecar sole-pusher, remove the vestigial cam-next self-narration). 2 advisory SUGGESTIONs deferred (prd.review.findings not reset on a no-findings round; buildFixStories iterates by verdict count vs findings.length). The reviewer-written review-report.json is gitignored like worker-report.json. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) still open. Operator to merge PR #69, then prune + rebuild/reinstall.

## cam/CAM-86-file-issue-on-main: file local issues to main without touching the work branch (commit-tree)

- **Started**: 2026-06-25
- **Closed**: 2026-06-25

- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #71, CI green 48s)
- **Summary**: Local filing (cam issue / /cam-issue create, none backend) used to edit issues.local.json and commit on the checked-out branch, entangling the backlog into feature branches and racing next_id. New deterministic createLocalIssueOnMain (src/commands/issue-file.ts) commits the new issue to main without checkout or touching the work branch (commit-tree via a temp index off-main, direct commit on-main), exposed as cam issue --file-local (stdin JSON), dispatched before the thin-proxy (mirrors CAM-72 ship-finalize). Plus the up-to-date-main guard + best-effort push (US-005) and read-from-main get/list (US-006). 7 stories, review CLEAN round 1, check:all green.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (7) over MVP (4), operator choice. commit-tree over a disposable worktree (fully injectable, hermetically testable). Read-from-main allocation is mandatory inside the primitive (fixes the next_id race); read-from-main for get/list is the display-consistency layer.
- **Blockers encountered**: The CAM-86 bug bit live during its own cycle. A parallel agent mis-filed the file-per-issue idea onto the branch as CAM-89, colliding with main's Conventional Commits CAM-89; relocated to main as CAM-90 (next_id 90 to 91) and rebased off the branch. The core primitive tripped biome complexity (25 vs 15, 159 lines) the reviewer does not run; refactored into 5 helpers post-review with argv-pinning tests holding. Concurrent operator git activity (a block-task-tools fix committed then reverted on the branch, landed properly on main as cff99a0) required reflog recovery. Operator first said no-force-push then chose option A: one --force-with-lease to publish the cleaned branch, then cam ship. file-size ceilings raised for test growth (_ref CAM-86).
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-90 (file-per-issue) is the structural fix that obsoletes this commit-tree workaround; when prioritized, the next_id/entanglement class disappears. Post-merge rebuild+reinstall so the installed cam carries --file-local (sidecar still 0.1.2). Process note: the orchestrator overreached by spawning a general-purpose Agent for the refactor (the role grants no Agent/Task; code work belongs to the implementer pane worker), the concern behind cff99a0.

## cam/CAM-91-agent-allowlist-hook — orchestrator Task/Agent allowlist via PreToolUse hook

- **Started**: 2026-06-26
- **Closed**: 2026-06-26
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-91-agent-allowlist-hook
- **Issue**: CAM-91
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #74, CI green, merged aa062b5)
- **Summary**: Replaces the cff99a0 blanket `disallowedTools` ban on Task/Agent (which also blocked the sanctioned planner/auditor, breaking the autonomous plan phase by construction: the first plan post-cff99a0 needed a manual override) with a deterministic PreToolUse allowlist hook (`.claude/hooks/orch-agent-allowlist.sh`) gating Task/Agent to {subagent-planner, subagent-auditor}; Edit/Write/NotebookEdit/AskUserQuestion stay blocked via `disallowedTools` so the orchestrator still cannot edit code. 7 stories (5 + 2 review-fix), 1392 pass / 0 fail, check:all green, review CLEAN round 3/3. The hook ships downstream via the embed/template system (US-004) and is documented in recovery-runbook scenario (q).
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (5) over MVP (3), operator choice. F-01 reconciliation chose Option A (add the `disallowedTools` block to the templates copy) over Option B (drop the block, hook-only): B would leave Edit/Write available, violating the orchestrator-never-edits-code invariant. This also converged a latent cff99a0 drift: the block existed only in the `.claude/` runtime mirror, never propagated to `templates/` + `_generated.ts`, so downstream `cam init` projects never received it. Hook reads subagent_type defensively across three field paths (`.tool_input.subagent_type // .tool_input.agent_type // .agent_type`) since the official doc is ambiguous; matcher `Task|Agent` hedges the trigger-tool-name uncertainty.
- **Blockers encountered**: Plan-time the auditor BLOCKed round 1 on 1 critical + 2 important, the exact two-layer-verification class CAM-91 exists to enforce: US-001 assumed the two orchestrator copies were symmetric mirrors (they were already out of sync), and two field-name oracles were tautological (`agent_type` is a substring of `subagent_type`, so `grep -q agent_type` passes even for a single-field read). All fixed + 3 suggestions folded, re-audit APPROVE. Review round 1 FIXES_PENDING:1 caught a CRITICAL the orchestrator had flagged live after US-002: the hook command was a bare relative path = fail-OPEN (a non-locatable hook errors non-2, which Claude Code treats as non-blocking and PROCEEDS), fixed to `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/...`; round 2 FIXES_PENDING:1 was doc-sync (runbook example still showed the unsafe path); round 3 CLEAN. The loop self-drove all 7 stories + 3 review rounds with ZERO manual re-arm, and the `[cam]` narrations reached the orchestrator pane live (CAM-70/78 holding end to end). Ship hygiene hit the recurring CAM-60 class: check:all file-size ratchet caught 3 files grown past ceiling (per-story worker runs only typecheck+tests); raised with `_ref=CAM-91`.
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-96 (jq fail-closed: the deny path exits non-2 if jq is absent, fail-open) and CAM-97 (S-4 live-capture of the real PreToolUse payload shape), both filed to main, reviewer-triaged non-blocking. No binary rebuild needed for the dogfood: the hook + settings.json + agent file are runtime-read from `.claude/` by Claude Code, not embedded in the cam binary; the next `cam run` after `git pull` activates the hook and the next `/cam-plan` will pass planner/auditor through the gate for real (rebuild matters only for downstream `cam init`, US-004). CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) hit again, killed by hand pre-ship; CAM-80 (worker-pane geometry, its pre-req) still open.

## cam/CAM-89-conventional-commits-version-bump: deterministic Conventional-Commits version bump (cam ship --bump + cam tag)

- **Started**: 2026-06-26
- **Closed**: 2026-06-26
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-89-conventional-commits-version-bump
- **Issue**: CAM-89
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #75, merged 4b6f407, tagged v0.2.0)
- **Summary**: Automates the version bump via Conventional Commits. `cam ship --bump` reads `git log main..HEAD`, classifies by pure regex (feat:MINOR, fix:PATCH, feat!/BREAKING:MAJOR signal), computes the next version under the 0.x convention (breaking maps to MINOR while major is 0; 1.0.0 never auto-emitted), writes src/version.ts + package.json + CHANGELOG.md, and commits `chore(release):` before push. `cam tag` is a separate post-merge command that tags main HEAD. 8 stories + 4 review-fixes, 1528 pass / 0 fail, review CLEAN round 2/3.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (9 stories) + Option A (file bump on branch, tag post-merge on main), operator choice. Option A is mandatory because the repo squash-merges: a branch-side tag would dangle off an unmerged SHA (verified: v0.2.0 landed on the real merged SHA 4b6f407). Classification source is the branch (main..HEAD), NOT v<lasttag>..HEAD on main (main commits are non-conventional CAM-NN/chore(cam)). Baseline is the version.ts literal, not the last tag (real drift: version.ts was 0.1.2, last tag v0.1.1).
- **Blockers encountered**: The loop self-drove 8 stories + review FIXES_PENDING:4 + 4 fixes + round-2 CLEAN with ZERO manual re-arm; the [cam] narrations reached the orchestrator pane live (CAM-70/78 holding). The two-layer review caught a real correctness bug (git add/commit exit status unchecked: spawnSync does not throw, so a pre-commit hook failure was swallowed and left files modified-but-uncommitted) and the CAM-53 class (US-007 event computed then discarded: writeEvent was never wired in production _buildBumpOpts, so the test-file grep oracle passed while the deliverable was dead). Both fixed round 1. Ship hygiene hit the recurring CAM-60 class: check:all caught a biome line-limit on runShipBump (extracted stageAndCommitBump helper) + 2 file-size ratchets raised with _ref CAM-89 (per-story worker runs only typecheck+tests).
- **Follow-ups**: rollChangelog inserts the new [X.Y.Z] section ABOVE the old [Unreleased] instead of renaming [Unreleased] inline, so a stale CAM-33 [Unreleased] body now sits below an empty fresh [Unreleased] (file a fix). CAM-84 (auto-merge) + CAM-71 (end-to-end autonomy) could fold `cam tag` into an auto-release post-merge step. Operator binary rebuilt+reinstalled to 0.2.0 (carries --bump/tag; the idle sidecar pid still runs old 0.1.2, harmless).

- **Summary**: /cam-ship Step 7 now runs `gh pr merge --auto --squash` right after `gh pr create`, best-effort: on failure (auto-merge or squash merging disabled in repo Settings) it prints a Settings > General > Pull Requests prerequisite hint and continues, since the PR already exists (US-001, both cam-ship.md copies + re-embedded _generated.ts + ordering-oracle regression test). The operator added the init/config prerequisite notice mid-plan (it is the issue spec own AVISO); the shared constant home is src/logging/notices.ts (leaf module, both call sites import without a cycle). Auto-merge is best-effort by design: it must never abort a ship whose PR already exists.
- **Blockers encountered**: None at runtime: the loop self-drove US-001 to US-004 + review CLEAN round 1 with ZERO manual re-arm; the [cam] narrations and the CLEAN verdict reached the orchestrator pane live (CAM-70/78 holding, 0.4.0 sidecar). Plan-time the auditor APPROVEd with 1 important (F-01: US-002 referenced a nonexistent SetupOptions writer seam, whereas printHint writes straight to stdout behind verifyAgent + collectSetupAnswers) plus 2 suggestions; F-01 (rewrite to a testable exported helper) and F-02 were applied by the planner before branching. Ship hygiene hit the recurring CAM-60 class: check:all file-size ratchet caught setup.ts (650) and cam-ship-hygiene.test.ts (50) past ceiling (per-story worker runs only typecheck+tests); raised with _ref CAM-84. cam tag ran via `bun index.ts tag` from source (installed cam 0.3.0 embeds its own version literal, stale-binary class).
- **Follow-ups**: 2 non-blocking reviewer SUGGESTIONs deferred: (1) cam init notice uses a raw stdout write while cam config uses the muted + quiet-gated printHint (parity nit, candidate follow-up); (2) the setup.ts/config.ts call-site wiring is not headlessly testable (Ink/readline + verifyAgent gate), so the helpers are unit-tested in isolation (PRD-accepted F-01 tradeoff). CAM-71 (end-to-end autonomy) now has its auto-merge prerequisite. CAM-73 (branch protection) still open: with it enabled, auto-merge would wait for required checks instead of merging immediately.

## cam/CAM-101-configurable-merge-mode — configurable cam ship merge mode (immediate | ci-gated)

- **Started**: 2026-06-26
- **Closed**: 2026-06-26
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-101-configurable-merge-mode
- **Issue**: CAM-101
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #78, merged 26e9bc4, tagged v0.5.0)
- **Summary**: Adds `[ship] merge_mode = "immediate" | "ci-gated"` (default immediate, current behavior preserved). ci-gated configures main branch protection via `gh api` (required check `ci`, strict=true, enforce_admins=false, required_pull_request_reviews=null) in cam init/config with a verify+warn fallback; the ship hands off after `gh pr create` + auto-merge (writing `.claude/.cam-merge-watch.json`) and the sidecar polls the PR for MERGED then runs a deterministic post-merge (pull main, tag, prune; NO journal, that stays orchestrator-authored). Absorbs CAM-73, unblocks CAM-71. 10 stories + 8 review-fix, 1657 pass / 0 fail.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (10) over MVP (5), operator choice. Journal stays orchestrator-authored (sidecar narrates pull/tag/prune only), preserving the documented invariant. Branch-protection helper uses the modern `checks:[{context}]` (the `contexts` array is deprecated). Operator accepted the deterministic verification (check:all green) at the review cap instead of a confirming round.
- **Blockers encountered**: Two-layer review rounds 1-2 caught 4 real production bugs (the ci-gated pipeline had no producer for the merge-watch trigger file; runPostMerge tagged the feature branch instead of main and could not delete the checked-out branch; OPEN+BLOCKED was treated as CI-red while it can mean checks still pending, false-stopping the happy path; US-008 events were not wired into the production runMergeWatch call), each closed with a real-git integration test, the fake-spawn-masks-dead-production class (CAM-53/89). Round 3 caught the recurring CAM-60 ship-hygiene class (file-size ratchet + biome complexity), fixed by raising ceilings (_ref CAM-101) and extracting helpers; the loop wedged at FIXES_PENDING:2 at the 3/3 cap (CAM-76), but the findings were deterministic gates verified green by `bun run check:all` directly, so the debt label is cosmetic. The result-event filesChanged came back empty for several stories (US-009, US-R3-001/002) though the commits were real; observability gap worth a note. At ship time branch protection was UNEXPECTEDLY already active on main (the exact CAM-101 ci-gated shape), so the immediate-mode ship behaved ci-gated: the PR went BEHIND (CAM-103 filed on main mid-cycle), update-branch made it BLOCKED on a pending `ci`, and it auto-merged only after CI passed (56s). This is the assincronia gotcha CAM-101 documents, and a live validation that the ci-gated premise holds; it falsifies the cam-ship-auto-merges-immediately memory premise.
- **Follow-ups**: CAM-103 (merge-watch blocks the sidecar outer loop without a session-health check, non-blocking SUGGESTION). US-010 (ci-gated live dogfood) deferred to post-merge rebuild+reinstall: the merge-watch lives in the merged code, not the 0.3.0 sidecar that drove this loop, so the live ci-gated post-merge (sidecar detecting MERGED, running pull/tag/prune autonomously) was NOT exercised here; this cycle's merge was completed by the orchestrator by hand. Binary rebuilt+reinstalled to 0.5.0 post-merge. CAM-65 (reviewer pane lingers post-CLEAN) not hit this cycle (review ended at the cap, not CLEAN).

## cam/CAM-100-automerge-notice-quiet-parity — quiet-gate cam init auto-merge notice + US-010 ci-gated dogfood

This cycle doubled as the CAM-101 US-010 dogfood: the FIRST live ci-gated ship.
- **Decisions**: Used CAM-100 as the US-010 vehicle (small, low-risk). merge_mode=ci-gated set via cam config (validated live: persisted [ship] merge_mode + configured branch protection). Accepted CAM-101's review FIXES_PENDING:2 debt via deterministic check:all-green verification (operator choice).

US-008 events watching->merged->post-merge-done fired. Procedural gotcha hit: the sidecar reads merge_mode ONCE at startup (sidecar.ts:348), so the sidecar respawned BEFORE cam config set ci-gated had runMergeWatchFn=undefined and did nothing; restarting it post-config fixed it (in a normal cam run this never bites: set mode, then cam run spawns the sidecar fresh). CAM-105 filed (post-merge-done event omits branchPrunedLocal/Remote; observability gap, the prune outcome is not recorded). Minor latent: post-merge prune uses `git branch -d` (safe) which worked here only because branch==upstream; could fail on a squash-merge if the tracking ref were pruned first (use -D). Binary at 0.6.0.

## cam/CAM-102-allowlist-hook-refino — allowlist hook refino (capability policy + CAM_SESSION scope + implementer anchoring)

- **Started**: 2026-06-26
- **Closed**: 2026-06-26
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-102-allowlist-hook-refino
- **Issue**: CAM-102
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #80, merged b146fd1, tagged v0.7.0)
- **Summary**: Refines the CAM-91 PreToolUse hook from a fixed name-allowlist to a capability policy (ALLOW read-only {Explore, Plan, claude-code-guide, planner, auditor, reviewer}, DENY code-writers {general-purpose, claude, fork, implementer} + absent/unknown) gated by a CAM_SESSION scope check (unset = exit 0/allow, interactive dev sessions unaffected), plus a flow-rule anchor in both orchestrator personas refusing implementer dispatch without a non-null issueNumber. Both hook copies + both personas kept byte-identical and re-embedded; runbook (q) rewritten. 5 stories (4 non-operator + 1 operator dogfood). 1668 pass / 0 fail, check:all green, review CLEAN round 1. The hook is runtime-read from .claude/ so the new policy went live this session the moment US-001 landed.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (5) over MVP (3), operator choice. Capability+scope live in the hook; implementer-issue/PRD anchoring is a persona flow rule, NOT a hook change (the hook only gates Task/Agent spawn). Security direction preserved: absent/misread subagent_type within cam scope errs to DENY.
- **Blockers encountered**: The sidecar was DEAD at cycle start (stale pid 77532 from a prior session, no supervisor lock); recovered in-place by starting `cam sidecar` standalone (no cam stop), 0.5.0 binary coherent since CAM-102 touches no src/supervisor. The loop then self-drove US-001..004 + review CLEAN round 1 with ZERO manual re-arm; [cam] narrations and the verdict reached the orchestrator live (CAM-70/78 holding). Plan-time the auditor BLOCKed twice (2 critical, the exact two-layer class: US-005 carried requires:"US-002" (a dependency overload) instead of the operator literal, which would have autonomously dispatched an operator-only dogfood; US-004 missed the real template persona at templates/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md, not the nonexistent templates/.claude/agents/, a silent embed drift that embed-vendor:check would not catch; 1 important: the test inherited ambient CAM_SESSION = green-local!=green-CI), all applied, re-audit APPROVE. US-005 operator dogfood verified live by the orchestrator: Explore spawn ALLOWED (was deny), general-purpose DENIED with the new capability message, CAM_SESSION-unset path ALLOWS all. ci-gated post-merge fully autonomous: CI gated the merge, the 0.5.0 sidecar detected MERGED and ran pull + tag v0.7.0 on the merged HEAD + prune, narrating with zero intervention.
- **Follow-ups**: Boot gap (process): the orchestrator cold-boot reads journal.md but not .cam-orch-handoff.consumed.json when it is newer than the last journal entry, so the end-of-session plan (plan CAM-102 next) was lost until the operator asked; candidate to teach the boot sequence to read the consumed handoff when newer than the latest journal entry. F-06 (US-001 AC#9 grep is structural only; AC#8 behavioral oracle carries the proof; non-blocking). CAM-96 (hook jq fail-closed) still open and now more relevant under the wider policy. CAM-104 (cam init non-interactive merge_mode hang, P1) still blocks build:release.

## cam/CAM-104-init-merge-mode-eof — non-interactive cam init merge_mode EOF hang (CAM-101 regression)

- **Started**: 2026-06-26
- **Closed**: 2026-06-26
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-104-init-merge-mode-eof
- **Issue**: CAM-104
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #81, merged e876d46, tagged v0.8.0)
- **Summary**: Fixes the P1 non-interactive `cam init` hang at the merge_mode prompt. Root cause: `ask`/`askChoice` (node:readline callback API) never resolved on EOF, so a piped/closed stdin hung the Promise forever. Fix: resolve to the default on the readline `close` event, plus an `input.readableEnded` short-circuit in `ask()` (a single EOF stream is not re-emitted across the sequential interfaces `collectViaReadline` creates). Also passes `--merge-mode immediate` in the build-release AC4 smoke, adds a deterministic collection-seam guard test, and documents the pattern in patterns.md. 5 stories (4 non-operator + 1 operator dogfood) + 1 review-fix. 1683 pass / 0 fail, check:all green, review CLEAN round 2.
- **Decisions**: Launch-ready (5) over MVP (3), operator choice. US-003 was reframed at plan time off a binary-spawn approach (would have skipped or failed on the claude-less CI runner, the verifyAgent short-circuit at setup.ts:517 returns before any prompt) to an in-process `collectViaReadline` EOF test, after the auditor BLOCKed it (F-01 critical / F-02 important, the CAM-53/89 two-layer class). US-005 (build:release) ran as an operator dogfood and passed clean.
- **Blockers encountered**: Review round 1 caught a real production gap the audit missed: the `rl.on('close')` guard only fired for the FIRST readline interface; subsequent interfaces on an already-ended single stream never re-receive `close`, so multi-undefined-option `cam init </dev/null` still hung. Fixed in US-R1-001 via the `readableEnded` short-circuit; round 2 CLEAN. check:all file-size ratchet caught setup.ts 697->726 (recurring CAM-60 class; per-story workers run only typecheck+tests), raised with _ref CAM-104. `cam ship --finalize` mis-parsed the none-backend string issueNumber "CAM-104" as integer 0 and closed nonexistent CAM-0, leaving CAM-104 open; closed by hand on the branch (main branch protection blocks a direct commit, so the close shipped via PR #81). ci-gated post-merge fully autonomous: CI gated the merge (mergeStateStatus BLOCKED = pending, not failed), the sidecar detected MERGED and ran pull + tag v0.8.0 + prune with zero intervention. The loop self-drove US-001..004 + review to CLEAN with zero manual re-arm.
- **Follow-ups**: File the `cam ship --finalize` none-backend bug (prefixed string issueNumber is parsed as integer 0; local-close should resolve prefixed string ids). Two reviewer SUGGESTIONs were addressed in US-R1-001 (all-undefined multi-prompt test + production-reachability note in patterns.md).

## cam/CAM-106-issue-schema-state-machine: issue schema + state machine + PRD-readiness gate (Epico A)

- **Started**: 2026-06-27
- **Closed**: 2026-06-27
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-106-issue-schema-state-machine
- **Issue**: CAM-106
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #82, merged d805df4, tagged v0.9.0)
- **Summary**: Foundation of the issue-system redesign (ADR 0001). Adds a typed issue state machine: stage (idea/specified/planned/shipped), status (open/abandoned; blocked is computed from the graph, never stored), blockedBy[] single source of truth, optional wsjf/rank/spec (populated by later epics). New modules src/issues/{types,graph,select}.ts: pure graph helpers (isBlocked/deriveBlocks/checkReferentialIntegrity) and selectPlannableIssue, which makes the PRD-readiness gate a unit-tested code invariant (D1) instead of orchestrator prose. cam-plan Step 2 no-arg now filters stage===specified; explicit cam plan CAM-N still plans an idea with a not-grill-specified warning (D2 transition bridge until Epico B). Live backlog migrated (76 closed to shipped, 37 open to idea, ad-hoc priority/state dropped, idempotent). issues.schema.json added (D3). 7 auto + 3 review-fix + 1 operator = 11 stories. 1743 pass, check:all green.
- **Decisions**: Operator-approved Epico A core slice (not the whole epic): gate-as-code, transition bridge, issues.schema.json, clean state/priority substitution. issueNumber kept integer 106 (CAM-113 workaround; finalize closed CAM-106, no phantom CAM-0). Migration committed on the branch so schema code + migrated data land atomically.
- **Blockers encountered**: Review two-layer caught real doc-vs-code drift across 3 rounds (FIXES_PENDING:2 to :1 to CLEAN): hallucinated graph helper names + wrong migration path + false migrated claim in the US-007 ADR note, a stale finalize docstring left by US-004 (the 2026-06-05 stale-comment class), and an inverted sort direction in the docs. Binary coherence: rebuilt+reinstalled cam from branch HEAD before ship so the installed finalize sets stage:shipped (not stale state:closed). check:all first run flaked on a race with the lingering reviewer pane (CAM-65 cosmetic), green 3x after. Orchestrator self-inflicted: an unquoted heredoc command-substituted backticks while writing the budget handoff, firing stray cam/git commands and corrupting the file (verified zero mutation: HEAD/reflog/main/tags intact); fixed by switching to quoted heredoc. Lesson: never write a file via unquoted heredoc when content has backticks or dollar-signs.
- **Follow-ups**: Filed via cam-issue: (1) scripts/cam/CLAUDE.md + subagent-auditor checklist claim no linter configured but biome + check:all lint are live (CAM-60), flagged by the auditor in F-02. (2) Deferred review SUGGESTIONs: selectPlannableFromFile returns undefined not null and swallows read errors; working-tree vs git-show-main source-of-truth seam in cam-plan; dead clock/ClockFn in ship-finalize; review findings array not cleared on a CLEAN verdict. Epicos B/C/D (CAM-107/108/109) now unblocked; CAM-107 (the grill) is the natural next plan since it promotes idea to specified so the no-arg gate has something to select.

## cam/CAM-107-grill-spec-layer — grill spec layer (/cam-spec + idea->specified promotion, Epico B)

- **Summary**: Epico B of the issue-system redesign. Deterministic writer `specifyIssueOnMain` (+ abandon/merge-into modes) commits to main via commit-tree, never touching the branch. New `src/issues/spec.ts` (typed Spec + validators), reviewer-behavioral oracle in the planner, CONTEXT.md/ADR location convention, production-wired `stage-promoted` event. 9 stories (8 auto + US-009 operator) + 2 review-fix. 1829 pass, check:all green, review CLEAN round 2.

Binary rebuilt+reinstalled (0.8.0 -> 0.9.0, then bump 0.10.0) so finalize is CAM-106-aware (CAM-107 -> stage:shipped). KEY GOTCHA: CAM-109/76/116-120 were filed direct-to-main during the cycle, making the branch's issues.local.json stale; merged origin/main into the branch BEFORE `cam ship --finalize` so finalize edited the CURRENT backlog and the squash-merge did not clobber main's filings (verified intact post-merge).
- **Follow-ups**: US-009 produced CAM-109 (`specified`: auto-default gateless pipeline, deterministic safety surface + Resend escalation, vertical-slicing, reviewer-behavioral) and filed CAM-116 (Epico D2: behavioral verification tooling), CAM-117 (plan phase as a deterministic sidecar runner), CAM-118 (orchestrator is read-only and cannot write CONTEXT.md/ADR; the grill's durable domain-layer output was NOT produced), CAM-119 (`cam init` does not install `templates/skills` downstream), CAM-120 (dedup jscpd). New CAM-121 candidate: harden `cam ship --finalize` to close the `none` issue on main via commit-tree, not the branch's stale working-tree copy (eliminates the clobber risk worked around this cycle).

## cam/CAM-109-plan-simplify-plan-approval — cam-plan plano unico + plan_approval (auto|operator) (Epico D)

- **Started**: 2026-06-27
- **Closed**: 2026-06-27
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-109-plan-simplify-plan-approval
- **Issue**: CAM-109
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #84, merged 4f87b9f, tagged v0.11.0)
- **Summary**: Epico D do redesign do issue-system. cam-plan vira plano unico proporcional a spec grelhada (mata o fork MVP-vs-launch-ready) com vertical-slicing; novo `[plan] plan_approval = auto | operator` (default auto), o unico gate humano pos-grill (auto roda plan->merge sem gate; auditor nos dois modos, BLOCK sempre trava). Safety surface deterministico em TS: decisao do branch, terminal duro de nao-convergencia (maxRounds -> MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT, absorve CAM-76/95), escalacao Resend best-effort (primeira dependencia externa de runtime, `src/notify/resend.ts`, chave via `RESEND_API_KEY` env). 10 stories (US-001..010) + 6 review-fix/hygiene, 1894 tests, check:all green.
- **Decisions**: plano unico proporcional sem fork (no espirito da propria issue); CRITICAL de design resolvido via opcao X (promover MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT antes de criar fix stories + terminal-sem-pending); re-review da refatoracao em vez de ship as cegas; Resend key via env (nao git-tracked), padrao LINEAR_API_KEY.
- **Blockers encountered**: o review do plano original (7 stories) NAO convergiu no round 3 (cap), revelando um CRITICAL de design: o auto-chain (US-005) derrotava o terminal de nao-convergencia (US-006), porque as fix stories do round terminal ficam passes:false antes da promocao do verdict, fazendo makeHasPendingStories ver false-pending e o auto-chain seguir pro ship. Resolvido por re-plan (US-008/009). Re-review (cap 3->5) pegou um gap de safety na US-007 (warnIfResendUnconfigured so checava apiKey, nao recipient) e o ship-hygiene recorrente CAM-60 (file-size 6 arquivos + biome complexity em runSidecar/ConfigScreen -> US-010). Convergiu CLEAN no round 3 do re-plan. O orquestrador pausou o loop 2x via hard-kill do sidecar: 1 legitima (CRITICAL de design, esperou o operador) e 1 precipitada (confundiu file-size-virando-finding com cauda longa disfuncional, mas o worker ja se auto-corrigia via raise+commit, pois workers tem Write).
- **Follow-ups**: licao de escopo: epico com 2 mudancas quase-independentes (cam-plan vs autonomia) deveria ter sido 2 PRs (D-plan limpo + D-auto, onde mora o risco e o conflito de design). Filado CAM-122 (cam journal append deterministico + fim do jq>budget ad-hoc: dar ao orquestrador um canal gateado de escrita de housekeeping sem Write irrestrito). D2 behavioral verification tooling (CAM-116) ainda gate a execucao dos oracles reviewer-behavioral emitidos. Journal e memorias deste ciclo escritos via Bash one-off autorizado pelo operador (o exato gap que CAM-122 resolve).

## cam/CAM-122-journal-append — cam journal append deterministico (orquestrador escreve housekeeping via comando)

- **Started**: 2026-06-27
- **Closed**: 2026-06-27
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-122-journal-append
- **Issue**: CAM-122
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #85, merged 8936a62, tagged v0.12.0)
- **Summary**: Implementa cam journal append: comando deterministico que renderiza e commita uma entry de ciclo no journal.md no main via commit-tree (familia issue-file/specify), sem tocar a branch. Le JSON estruturado do stdin e GARANTE o formato (validacao de 8 campos required incl. title, normalizacao de em-dash so no corpo, dedup por cycleId com --force, push best-effort). E o canal de escrita gateado que deixa o orquestrador read-only persistir o journal sem o jq-redirect/Write ad-hoc. 3 stories + 6 review-fix, 1928 tests, check:all green.
- **Decisions**: Escopo journal-only (budget/memoria/archive ficam no CAM-123). JSON estruturado com render pelo comando (formato garantido pelo codigo, nao pela disciplina do orquestrador). commit-tree-to-main (familia issue-file/specify, elimina a classe de clobber CAM-121). Push best-effort + invocacao manual no cycle-close (o conteudo do journal e sintese do orquestrador, nao gerado pelo ship/sidecar).
- **Blockers encountered**: Review nao convergiu CLEAN em 3 rounds (cap): cada round pegou um bug real diferente num comando que toca git plumbing (labels divergentes do corpus, doc/schema arrays-vs-string, swallowed git-show error, teste tautologico do dispatch, title validation gap), zero re-incidencia (2,2,1,1). O 0.9.0 dispatchou um 4o fix (bug CAM-76) que ironicamente ajudou; o orquestrador raised o cap pra um confirming round (precedente CAM-78) que retornou CLEAN apos o title fix. Ship-hygiene limpo (worker resolveu file-size/jscpd via story no loop, lico orch-no-hardkill).
- **Follow-ups**: Filados CAM-123 (consolidacao knowledge-layer) e CAM-124 (extrair helper on-main da 3a copia da commit-tree plumbing). 2 suggestions non-blocking deferidas (non-null-object guard no JSON.parse do dispatch; dedup nao pega entries legadas com separador dois-pontos). Licao de escopo: jobSize 3 subestimou a superficie de bug de um comando de git plumbing (4+ rounds de review). Este proprio journal foi escrito pelo cam journal append 0.12.0 (dogfood: a feature fechando o ciclo sobre si mesma).

## cam/CAM-108-triage-deterministic-rank — Epico C Slice 1: triage deterministico (Kahn topo-sort + WSJF + cam triage)

- **Started**: 2026-06-28
- **Closed**: 2026-06-28
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-108-triage-deterministic-rank
- **Issue**: CAM-108
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #87, merged, tagged v0.14.0)
- **Summary**: Slice 1 (deterministic core) of Epico C, cross-list backlog reprioritization. Adds a hand-rolled Kahn topological-sort plus WSJF ranking module (src/issues/rank.ts) that computes a dense 1-based rank over {stage:specified, status:open}; a graph gate (Kahn cycle detection plus reuse of graph.ts checkReferentialIntegrity) that hard-fails transactionally on cycle/missing-id/self-ref and soft-warns on cross-stage blockers and missing/zero WSJF; and a deterministic cam triage CLI that reads the backlog from main, computes, gates, writes the rank field via commit-tree, is idempotent (no-op skips the commit), and emits CAM_TRIAGE_RANKED/CAM_TRIAGE_REJECTED sentinels. US-001 extracted the shared on-main commit-tree helper (src/git/on-main.ts) from its 3 verbatim copies (issue-file, issue-specify, journal), closing CAM-124 and keeping the jscpd dup gate green. 5 stories, 1992 pass / 0 fail, check:all green, review CLEAN round 1. The loop self-drove US-001 to US-005 to review with zero manual re-arm.
- **Decisions**: Grilled the epic and SLICED it: Slice 1 = deterministic core (this PR); Slice 2 (subagent-triager LLM cross-list reconciler, auto-trigger on idea-to-specified promotion, diff auto-narration, persistent operator overrides, Resend escalation) filed as CAM-127, blockedBy CAM-108. Operator chose option (b) at grill time: extract the on-main commit-tree helper as part of CAM-108 (closes CAM-124) rather than adding a 4th verbatim copy that would trip the jscpd dup gate. Ranking universe is {specified, open} only (planned is a defined-but-unreachable stage today). WSJF = SAFe CoD/JobSize; missing or jobSize<=0 degrades to WSJF 0 plus warn, never crash. Gate is transactional: on hard-fail keep prior rank and exit 1; on no-change skip the commit.
- **Blockers encountered**: The CAM-123 reviewer pane lingered post-CLEAN (CAM-65) and the 2-pane mutex refused cam next; killed pane %9 by hand, then cam next re-armed and the CAM-57 self-heal spawned a fresh worker pane %10. The whole 5-story loop plus review self-drove with zero re-arm and every [cam] push reached the orchestrator live (CAM-70/78 holding). Sidecar (started standalone at CAM-123 boot, pid 8573) drove both CAM-123 and CAM-108. The installed cam is stale 0.12.0 (predates the triage command), so the cam triage smoke ran from source via bun index.ts (0 specified, clean no-op, CAM_TRIAGE_RANKED=0 sha=none); the sidecar coherence was unaffected since CAM-108 touches no src/supervisor or agents.
- **Follow-ups**: Rebuild plus reinstall the cam binary to expose the new cam triage command to the installed CLI (0.12.0 lacks it). 3 non-blocking reviewer SUGGESTIONs filed as a follow-up issue: triage warning-source asymmetry (no-op vs commit path), noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity suppressed for rank.ts instead of refactoring runKahn, and an unused reserved RunTriageOptions.clock field. CAM-124 (on-main helper extraction) was completed by US-001 but is still stage:idea on the backlog: there is no cam issue close CLI for an arbitrary issue (finalize closes only the PRD issueNumber), so CAM-124 is recorded here as absorbed by CAM-108 and left for the operator to close or a future close-command to retire. Slice 2 = CAM-127.

## cam/CAM-90-file-per-issue — file-per-issue robust cutover do issue-system local (mata next_id + multi-writer + branch-entanglement)

- **Started**: 2026-06-28
- **Closed**: 2026-06-28
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-90-file-per-issue
- **Issue**: CAM-90
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #88, merged, tagged v0.15.0)
- **Summary**: Full robust cutover of the local issue system from the monolithic scripts/cam/issues.local.json (array + next_id counter) to one file per issue at scripts/cam/issues/CAM-NNNN.json (4-digit padded filename, unpadded id field). New primitives: on-main helper extended to multi-file atomic commit + compare-and-swap update-ref + bounded retry (hardens ALL on-main writers vs silent concurrent-commit loss); readBacklogFromMain (git ls-tree + single cat-file --batch, numeric sort, read-from-main invariant); allocateId (max-on-main + 1, CAS re-allocation). All 4 writers + the selectPlannableFromFile reader converted; schema + docs (dual-copy) + embed updated. 10 stories + 1 review-fix, 2060 pass / 0 fail, check:all green, review CLEAN round 2. The 129-issue backlog migrated intact (verified on main: 129 unique ids, CAM-1..CAM-129 present, CAM-90 itself shipped as CAM-0090.json); no-vestiges grep over the live surface returns 0 outside the 4 documented exceptions.
- **Decisions**: NO slicing (operator: the most robust way even if longer). OPTION B: the migration EXECUTES ON THE BRANCH (writes 129 files + deletes issues.local.json, committed on the branch) so the branch tree is the complete self-consistent migrated state and the squash-merge is a plain tree replacement, instead of the planner default of commit-tree-to-main at finalize (which would have shipped an empty branch dir and coupled backlog survival to finalize ordering = backlog-loss risk). Filename 4-digit padded, id field unpadded (numeric sort, not lexical). Operator-mandated no-vestiges invariant: zero issues.local.json/next_id references on the live surface, with 4 documented exceptions (append-only history, issue-content fields, the migration module, historical CHANGELOG). CAS update-ref hardens every on-main writer. Closes the CAM-124 helper lineage; obsoletes CAM-121 (finalize commit-tree hardening) and the CAM-113 class (string issueNumber parsed as 0).
- **Blockers encountered**: TWO design/correctness CRITICALs, both caught and fixed before merge. (1) Mid-loop the orchestrator paused (hard-kill of the sidecar) after US-006: the PRD migrated to main via commit-tree at finalize while US-007 only deleted issues.local.json from the branch, so the branch would have shipped an EMPTY issues/ dir and the 129 issues could be lost if finalize did not run from source in the right order; operator chose Option B, the PRD was re-planned (US-007 now executes the migration on the branch), the US-007 WIP was discarded, and the loop resumed clean. (2) Review round 1 returned FIXES_PENDING:1 on a real CRITICAL the two-layer review existed to catch: parseBatchOutput in readBacklogFromMain sliced the git cat-file --batch payload by CHAR length but <size> is BYTES, so multi-byte UTF-8 (the repo's Portuguese accents) over-read and silently dropped 38/129 issues, which could reintroduce the exact id-collision bug CAM-90 kills; all unit/integration fixtures were ASCII-only (byteLength === length) so it slipped every gate. Fixed to byte-accurate Buffer slicing + accented fixtures (US-R1-001); round 2 CLEAN. Process: the planner subagent hit an API connection drop mid-amendment (its write did not land); re-sent and confirmed on disk. cam ship --finalize was run from source (bun index.ts), not the installed old-format binary, so CAM-90 closed in the new per-file format.
- **Follow-ups**: REBUILD + REINSTALL the cam binary (installed is 0.14.0, OLD array format). CRITICAL until then: do NOT file any issue via the installed cam (it would write issues.local.json, reintroducing a vestige); file from source (bun index.ts issue --file-local) or wait for the rebuild. CAM-121 and CAM-113 are now obsolete (CAM-90 subsumes them); close them after rebuild (or via a one-off in the new per-file format). The migration module scripts/cam/migrate-issues-schema.ts persists as the downstream migration path for cam init projects still on the old format. Lesson worth capturing: ASCII-only test fixtures masked a bytes-vs-chars bug in a git cat-file --batch parser; any parser over git --batch output must slice the raw Buffer by byte, and tests must include multi-byte content.

## cam/CAM-133-on-main-writer-ref-only — on-main writers always ref-only (fix commitOnMain stale-index corruption)

- **Started**: 2026-06-28
- **Closed**: 2026-06-28
- **Branch**: cam/CAM-133-on-main-writer-ref-only
- **Issue**: CAM-133
- **Outcome**: shipped (PR #89, merged 1d9b3c7, tagged v0.16.0)
- **Summary**: Fixes a P1 on-main writer corruption found LIVE during the CAM-110 spec cycle: specifying CAM-110 silently dropped two just-filed child issues (CAM-131/132). Root cause: specifyIssueOnMain (specify/abandon/merge), journal append, and triage used the branchWasMain ? commitOnMain : commitTreeToMain split; on main, commitOnMain commits the local working-tree index, which goes stale after a prior ref-only writeIssueFile (CAM-90), reverting sibling files. CAM-90 CAS protects concurrent writers advancing the ref, not a stale local index vs the ref. Fix: eliminate the split at all 6 call sites, always use commitTreeToMain (tree from read-tree main, never the stale index), remove the orphaned commitOnMain. All on-main writers now symmetric and ref-only. 3 stories + 3 review-fix, 2060 pass, check:all green (no ratchet raise: net code removal), review CLEAN round 3.
- **Decisions**: Approach (a) eliminate commitOnMain over (b) resync-before-commit, operator choice: (a) removes an inconsistency (writeIssueFile is already ref-only since CAM-90) instead of adding resync complexity, and no caller needs the working-tree update. The whole loop self-drove plan to implement to review to ship to merge with zero manual re-arm (CAM-58/70/75/78 holding live); ci-gated post-merge fully autonomous (CI green, auto-merge, sidecar pull/tag v0.16.0/prune).
- **Blockers encountered**: The bug was discovered by the orchestrator itself: filing CAM-131/132 (ref-only) then specifying CAM-110 (commitOnMain) on main dropped the two children; recovered by hand (git checkout of the originals + commit a69cf13) and filed as CAM-133. Auditor APPROVEd at the 3-important boundary (all one root cause: ~6 pre-existing on-main tests invalidated by the cutover were not pre-empted in story notes); folded the findings into the PRD via the planner before branching. Review caught residual cutover dead code over 3 rounds (FIXES_PENDING:2, :1, CLEAN): round 1 dead writeFile local + stale comments, round 2 unused branchWasMain destructure, all mechanical, zero re-incidence. Journal append ran from source (bun index.ts) since the installed binary predates the fix.
- **Follow-ups**: (1) Pre-existing dead code buildMergeDescription in issue-specify.ts (defined, never called), out of PR scope, file a surgical cleanup. (2) knip gap: noUnusedLocals/noUnusedParameters:false + knip.json exclude let residual dead locals slip past static gates (the F-05 class), forcing the reviewer to catch them over 3 rounds; consider a static gate for unused locals/params. (3) CAM-110 umbrella now blockedBy CAM-131 (handoff cycle-boundary) + CAM-132 (meta-loop inter-ciclo), both idea; drain of specified plannables complete (only CAM-110 remains, blocked). (4) Rebuild+reinstall the cam binary to 0.16.0 (carries the fix); until then file via bun index.ts. (5) CAM-131 invariant dogfooded live: orchestrator budget went over mid-cycle and the handoff was correctly deferred to this cycle boundary.

Salva tokens-por-ciclo no event log (kind cycle-tokens) e conserta a bomba do cap de respawn do CAM-23 (reset em reason=cycle-close). 4 stories (US-001..004) + 1 review-fix (US-R1-001), 2082 pass, check:all verde, review CLEAN round 2. Primeira fatia concreta do epico CAM-110.
- **Decisions**: Gatilho REDEFINIDO pelo operador no grill: NAO threshold, mas handoff incondicional por ciclo de implementacao; o orch-budget vira so reporter de custo + fonte dos dados-por-ciclo. Ponto de arme = appendJournalEntryOnMain (garante journal+backlog+event-log persistidos antes do reset). O cap de respawn reseta em reason=cycle-close (backstop anti-loop preservado p/ respawns sem progresso).
